# Skills · R · part 9

898 entries, readmedaddy to recall. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **readmedaddy**: Use when writing or improving a README — thin, stub, outdated, a wall of text, unscannable, or missing; a new repo needs a front page; no quickstart, install, badges, or examples; broken instructions or AI slop. Triggers include "write me a README", "make this README better", "the readme sucks", "polish the readme", "readme.md", "project front page", "documentation landing". Works for any repo: CLI, library, framework, app/SaaS, infra, data/ML, agent skill, research, monorepo, internal tool. Yields to user and project instructions; never invents facts about the code. ([Systemartis/readmedaddy](https://github.com/Systemartis/readmedaddy/tree/HEAD/skills/readmedaddy) · ★8)
- **readmes**: Use when a repository's directories have no README or a stale one and the next reader (human or agent) would have to infer what each directory is for - the map already knows, and wawe-readmes drafts the docs it is missing. ([ngavrish/where-are-we](https://github.com/ngavrish/where-are-we/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/readmes) · ★2)
- **readonly**: User-invoked modifier for evidence-first read-only investigation. Use when the user appends /readonly to a question or another skill request: answer by checking the codebase and available read-only sources, do not implement, do not open issues, do not mutate local or remote state, and do not guess with 'likely' or 'probably' when evidence can be gathered. ([CheckPickerUpper/skills](https://github.com/CheckPickerUpper/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/engineering/readonly) · ★6)
- **readonly**: Use when reasoning about readonly — A schema.org component word, collided out of schema.org compounds — fused from readonlyValue (sti · collapse · merge). ([erpax/erpax](https://github.com/erpax/erpax/tree/HEAD/src/vocabulary/readonly) · ★2)
- **readonly-attested-post-probe**: 验证声明 read_only 风险的语义只读 POST 免运行时审批执行。用户提到“只读声明 POST 探针”、对应英文名称或要求运行该验收 workflow 时使用。 ([ChronoAIProject/nyx-skills](https://github.com/ChronoAIProject/nyx-skills/tree/HEAD/readonly-attested-post-probe))
- **readonly-scope**: Interpret user-requested read-only work as protecting Git-tracked files while allowing commands and writes outside the Git index. ([jleechanorg/jleechan-skills](https://github.com/jleechanorg/jleechan-skills/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/readonly-scope) · ★4)
- **readonly-stateflow-cold-to-hot**: Expose state through `asStateFlow()` rather than an upcast of the mutable holder, and turn a cold source flow hot with `stateIn(scope, WhileSubscribed(timeout), initial)` so it survives collector churn without running forever. Use when a screen re-queries its source on every rotation or navigation, when work continues after the last collector leaves, or when something outside the owner is writing state it should only be reading. ([maxrave-dev/kotlin-footguns](https://github.com/maxrave-dev/kotlin-footguns/tree/HEAD/skills/readonly-stateflow-cold-to-hot) · ★563)

The work runs in a child agent so the main conversation's context stays clean. Use whenever the user invokes /readout, says "wri ([warpdotdev/common-skills](https://github.com/warpdotdev/common-skills) · ★580 · 15k installs)

- **readout**: Write replies and work reports a person can take in on one read — one claim per sentence, action in the verb, uncertainty attached to the claim it belongs to. Use for /readout, "make your reports readable", "your output is hard to read", "stop writing like an AI", or when a run's reporting style needs fixing. The body below is standalone prose — paste it into AGENTS.md or CLAUDE.md to apply it every turn instead of on invocation. ([keli-wen/dev-skills](https://github.com/keli-wen/dev-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/readout) · ★11)
- **readout**: 写出别人一遍就能读懂的回复和工作汇报：一句话只说一件事，动作放在动词里，不确定性绑在它所属的那条结论上。当用户说 /readout、「你的汇报太难读了」「说人话」「别写得像 AI」，或者需要修正某次运行的汇报风格时使用。下面的正文独立成篇：整段贴进 AGENTS.md 或 CLAUDE.md，就能每轮生效，不必调用。 ([keli-wen/dev-skills](https://github.com/keli-wen/dev-skills/tree/HEAD/skills-zh/readout) · ★11)

- **readtime**: PM ritual to acknowledge an unread Worker handoff. Lists unread handoff pointers in `&lt;cwd&gt;/.claude/inbox/pm/` (i.e., files without a `&lt;!-- PM:READ:YYYY-MM-DD --&gt;` marker), lets the user pick one, opens the canonical §6 handoff for review, then appends the PM:READ marker to the pointer so it's filtered out of future SessionStart and `/check-handoffs` surfacing. Requires an open PM session (`&lt;cwd&gt;/.claude/pm-session.txt`). Companion to `/check-handoffs` (lists without acknowledging) and the SessionStart auto-discovery hook. ([earlyprototype/early-prototype](https://github.com/earlyprototype/early-prototype/tree/HEAD/plugins/early-prototype/skills/readtime))

Use this skill for summarizing long-form content, creating study materials, surveying research threads, or "weaving" multiple sources into a unified intellectual map. Use when user asks about articles saved, highlights created, or most-highlighted content for today, yesterday, last week, last month, or custom date ranges (e.g., "show my Readwise activity from Jan 1-7"). Requires READWISE_API_TOKEN env var. Connects to Readwise Highlights API (v2) and Reader API (v3). - **ready-check**: The PM-side readiness gate — "airport security for PRDs." A PM (or anyone) gives a PRD as any source — JIRA / Linear / Notion / Confluence / Google Doc / Slack link / GitHub issue / Figma URL, pasted text, an image, or a plain description — and the agent scores it against the Definition of Ready, surfaces the blockers the PM can fix themselves in plain language, and (when ready) mints a tamper-evident gate code + a clean hand-off for dev. Runs identically in Claude Code and Cowork. Use when the user says "ready check", "is this PRD ready", "check my PRD before dev", "gate this requirement", o… ([iampawan/Manifest](https://github.com/iampawan/Manifest/tree/HEAD/skills/ready-check) · ★5)
- **ready-check**: Validate implementation readiness before execution — checks spec completeness, architecture decisions, dependencies, test strategy, and acceptance criteria. Returns PASS/CONCERNS/FAIL verdict. Use after planning, before coding. ([avogiatzis/claude-plugins](https://github.com/avogiatzis/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/team-workflow/skills/ready-check))
- **ready-check-ticket**: Use when asked whether a Jira ticket is Ready or safe to proceed/implement, to "gate" a ticket, or for an independent second opinion on a ticket's readiness. Runs the FULL gated review of ONE ticket — a type-aware analysis plus a blind independent critic — and reconciles them into a single PASS/BLOCKED verdict. Read-only. NOT for a plain single-ticket summary (use analyze-ticket), and NOT for walking an epic's child issues (that is a separate feature). Triggers on readiness/gate intent, never on a bare "analyze". ([Erchamion/claude-extensions](https://github.com/Erchamion/claude-extensions/tree/HEAD/plugins/jira-cloud-tools/skills/ready-check-ticket))
- **ready-for-pr**: Run requested local CI gates, fix in-scope failures, and report readiness. Mark an existing draft ready only when that action is authorized and its published head contains the validated state. Ordinary implementation or PR metadata work does not trigger this skill. ([calmtechltd/calm-craft](https://github.com/calmtechltd/calm-craft/tree/HEAD/skills/ready-for-pr) · ★2)
- **ready-for-pr**: Run the same quality gates CI runs — types, lint, dead code, tests — and fix what fails until the branch would pass. Then mark the current branch's GitHub PR ready for review if it is still a draft. Use when the user says "ready for PR", "run the checks", "is this ready to ship", "will CI pass", or before opening a pull request. A bare commit or submit does not run this suite and must not mark the PR ready. ([calmtechltd/calmtech-marketplace](https://github.com/calmtechltd/calmtech-marketplace/tree/HEAD/plugins/calm-craft/skills/ready-for-pr))
- **ready-for-review**: Use when the user says "ready-for-review", "ready for review", "prepare for review", or "finalize for review", or when work is ready for PR review handoff. Bookend to kickoff: gates, review, final check, and PR handoff. Do NOT use for mid-implementation commits or unfinished work. Reads workflow.md and probes capabilities lazily. ([sandsower/beislid](https://github.com/sandsower/beislid/tree/HEAD/skills/ready-for-review) · ★10)
- **ready-for-review**: Pre-handoff gate: runs verification, code review, syncs or opens a PR. DO NOT TRIGGER when: no push or gh pr ready is attempted, or on the default branch. ([jcdendrite/claude-config](https://github.com/jcdendrite/claude-config/tree/HEAD/claude-skills/skills/ready-for-review) · ★4)
- **ready-for-review**: Rewrite an existing draft PR's title and body from scratch, then mark it ready for review. ([tgvashworth/agent-plugins](https://github.com/tgvashworth/agent-plugins/tree/HEAD/utils/skills/ready-for-review) · ★1)

- **ready-merge**: WSS-iOS-V2에서, PR을 올린 뒤 머지 직전에 작업 브랜치를 최신 develop 위로 rebase하고 --force-with-lease로 원격 PR을 갱신해 "머지 가능 상태"로 만들 때 사용한다. ① preflight 안전 확인 → ② origin/develop 위로 rebase(충돌 시 멈추고 사용자와 해결) → ③ (선택) tuist build로 전체 모듈 빌드 검증 → ④ 승인 후 force-with-lease push, 까지 한다. 실제 머지 버튼은 사람이 GitHub에서 누른다(이 스킬은 머지하지 않는다). "머지 준비", "develop에 리베이스", "force push로 PR 갱신", 또는 "/ready-merge" 같은 요청에 트리거. ⚠️ force-push는 외부로 나가는 비가역 작업 → 승인 후에만 실행한다. ([Team-WSS/WSS-iOS-V2](https://github.com/Team-WSS/WSS-iOS-V2/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/ready-merge) · ★3)
- **ready-to-fight**: 사용자가 제시한 주장·계획·기술 산출물을 정면으로 반박하고 서로 근거를 요구하며 입장 변화를 추적하는 상태형 적대적 숙의 스킬입니다. "ready-to-fight로 검증해줘", red team·악마의 대변인·반론 토론 요청에 명시적으로 사용하고, 의사결정이 걸린 검증 가능한 업무 주장에는 안전 경계를 확인한 뒤 암시적으로 사용합니다. 개인 경험·감정 지원·트라우마·의료·법률·재무 등 고위험 조언이나 의도가 모호한 대화에는 자동 호출하지 않습니다. Use when the user asks "do you remember...", "what did I tell you about...", "check your memory", or when starting work that likely has stored context (preferences, project constraints, past decisions) not found in the repo. Keyword-relevant memories are auto-injected per prompt by a hook; use this skill for deliberate or semantic (meaning-based) lookups. - **recall**: Manually query the second-brain (Trilium) for notes relevant to the current task. ([wrxck/matts-second-brain](https://github.com/wrxck/matts-second-brain/tree/HEAD/skills/recall) · ★1)

- **recall**: Deliberately recall what memory knows — answer "what do you remember about X", "what do you know here", or "why was that injected", and list the corpus by type. Triggers include "what do you remember about", "what do you know here", "recall", "/hippo:recall". The read verb between /hippo:new (write) and /hippo:audit (maintenance); not plumbing (that's /hippo:doctor).
